Apply ( [B.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A2.Ex34 "Proof of Theorem˜2.1. ‣ B.1 Proof of Theorem˜2.1 ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")) to hh and h(T)h^{(T)}, we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | \|err(T)​(h)−err(T)​(h(T))−𝐄\[1W​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)\]\|≤2​W​Δ.\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h)-\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h^{(T)})-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\bigg\[\frac{1}{W}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)\bigg\]\bigg\|\leq 2W\Delta. |  |

By ( [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A2.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ Proof of Theorem˜2.1. ‣ B.1 Proof of Theorem˜2.1 ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")), we have 1W​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h^(i))≤0\frac{1}{W}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(\hat{h}^{(i)})\leq 0, which means

|     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  |  | err(T)​(h^(i))−err(T)​(h(T))\displaystyle\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(\hat{h}^{(i)})-\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h^{(T)}) |  |
|  | ≤\displaystyle\leq\; | 2​W​Δ+𝐄\[1W​∑t=(i−1)​W+1Tdiffj​(h^(i))\]−1W​∑t=(i−1)​W+1Tdiffj​(h^(i))\displaystyle 2W\Delta+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\bigg\[\frac{1}{W}\sum\_{t=(i-1)W+1}^{T}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(\hat{h}^{(i)})\bigg\]-\frac{1}{W}\sum\_{t=(i-1)W+1}^{T}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(\hat{h}^{(i)}) |  |
|  | ≤\displaystyle\leq\; | 2​W​Δ+\|1W​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h^(i))−𝐄\[1W​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h^(i))\]\|.\displaystyle 2W\Delta+\bigg\|\frac{1}{W}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(\hat{h}^{(i)})-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\bigg\[\frac{1}{W}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(\hat{h}^{(i)})\bigg\]\bigg\|. |  | (3) |

To get the desired error rate, we will next show that with high probability, for every h∈ℋh\in\mathcal{H}, 1W​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)\frac{1}{W}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h) is close to its expectation. Denote by σj2​(h)=𝐄\[diffj2​(h)\]=𝐏𝐫(x(t),y(t))∼D(t)\[h​(x(t))≠h(T)​(x(t))\]\sigma^{2}\_{j}(h)=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\[\mathrm{diff}^{2}\_{j}(h)\]=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(x^{(t)},y^{(t)})\sim D^{(t)}}\[h(x^{(t)})\neq h^{(T)}(x^{(t)})\] and V​(h):=W−1​∑j=1Wσj2​(h)V(h):=W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\sigma^{2}\_{j}(h). We first develop a V​(h)V(h)-dependence bound for all h∈Hh\in H.

For r∈(0,1)r\in(0,1), we denote by H​(r):={h∈ℋ∣V​(h)≤r}H(r):=\{h\in\mathcal{H}\mid V(h)\leq r\}. Notice that for every h∈H​(r)h\in H(r), we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | W−1∑j=1W𝐄(diffj(h)−𝐄diffj(h))2≤r.W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}(\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h))^{2}\leq r. |  |

Define random variable Z​(r):=suph∈H​(r)\|W−1​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)−W−1​𝐄\[∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)\]\|Z(r):=\sup\_{h\in H(r)}\left\|W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)-W^{-1}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\bigg\[\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)\bigg\]\right\|. By Talagrand’s inequality \[ [Tal96](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#bib.bibx39 "")\], for any δr∈(0,1),\delta\_{r}\in(0,1), we have with probability 1−δr1-\delta\_{r}

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | Z​(r)≤𝐄Z​(r)+2​r​log⁡(1/δr)W+3​log⁡(1/δr)W.\displaystyle Z(r)\leq\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}Z(r)+\sqrt{\frac{2r\log(1/\delta\_{r})}{W}}+\frac{3\log(1/\delta\_{r})}{W}. |  |

It remains to upper bound 𝐄Z​(r)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}Z(r). By Theorem 8 in \[ [BM02](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#bib.bibx8 "")\], we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄Z​(r)≤2​𝐄x,y𝐄s​suph∈V​(r)W−1​∑j=1Wsj​diffj​(h),\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}Z(r)\leq 2\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x,y}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{s}\sup\_{h\in V(r)}W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}s\_{j}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h), |  |

where s∈{±1}Ws\in\{\pm 1\}^{W} is an independent Rademacher vector. To upper bound the Rademacher complexity 𝐄x,y𝐄s​suph∈V​(r)W−1​∑j=1Wsj​diffj​(h)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x,y}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{s}\sup\_{h\in V(r)}W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}s\_{j}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h), we define a metric between hypotheses in V​(r)V(r). For h,h′∈H​(r)h,h^{\prime}\in H(r), we define θ2​(h,h′):=𝐄W−1​∑j=1W(diffj​(h)−diffj​(h′))2\theta^{2}(h,h^{\prime}):=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\left(\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)-\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h^{\prime})\right)^{2}. Notice that θ2​(h,h′)≤2​r\theta^{2}(h,h^{\prime})\leq 2r by the definition of H​(r)H(r).
For every hh, we define Xh:=W−1​∑j=1Wsj​diffj​(h)X\_{h}:=W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}s\_{j}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h). Notice that Xh−Xh′X\_{h}-X\_{h^{\prime}} has sub-Gaussian increment with respect to θ​(h,h′)/W\theta(h,h^{\prime})/\sqrt{W}. By Dudley’s inequality,we have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄​suph∈H​(r)Xh≤1W​∫02​rlog⁡𝒩​(ϵ,H​(r),θ)​𝑑ϵ=O​(d​r​log⁡r−1W),\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\sup\_{h\in H(r)}X\_{h}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{W}}\int\_{0}^{\sqrt{2r}}\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,H(r),\theta)d\epsilon=O\left(\sqrt{\frac{dr\log r^{-1}}{W}}\right), |  | (4) |

where 𝒩​(ϵ,H​(r),θ)\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,H(r),\theta) is the covering number under metric θ\theta and the last equality follows the VC dimension of HH.
This gives with probability 1−δr1-\delta\_{r},

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | suph∈H​(r)\|W−1​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)−W−1​𝐄\[∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)\]\|≤O​(d​r​log⁡r−1+2​r​log⁡(1/δr)W+3​log⁡(1/δr)W).\displaystyle\sup\_{h\in H(r)}\left\|W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)-W^{-1}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\bigg\[\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)\bigg\]\right\|\leq O\left(\sqrt{\frac{dr\log r^{-1}+2r\log(1/\delta\_{r})}{W}}+\frac{3\log(1/\delta\_{r})}{W}\right). |  |

By choosing rk=2−k,k=1,2,…r\_{k}=2^{-k},k=1,2,\dots, δrk=δ/2k+1\delta\_{r\_{k}}=\delta/2^{k+1}, and union bound, we have with probability 1−δ1-\delta for every h∈Hh\in H,

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | W−1​∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)−W−1​𝐄\[∑j=1Wdiffj​(h)\]=O​(dV(h)logV(h)−1+2V(h)log(1/(δ)W+3log(1/(V(h)δ)W).\displaystyle W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)-W^{-1}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\bigg\[\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)\bigg\]=O\left(\sqrt{\frac{dV(h)\log V(h)^{-1}+2V(h)\log(1/(\delta)}{W}}+\frac{3\log(1/(V(h)\delta)}{W}\right). |  | (5) |

It remains to analyze V​(h)V(h).We have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | err(t)​(h)−err(t)​(h(T))\displaystyle\mathrm{err}^{(t)}(h)-\mathrm{err}^{(t)}(h^{(T)}) | ≥err(t)​(h)−err(t)​(h(t))−2​Δ​W\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{err}^{(t)}(h)-\mathrm{err}^{(t)}(h^{(t)})-2\Delta W |  |
|  |  | =𝐄(x,y)∼D(t)𝟙​{h​(x)≠h(t)​(x)}​(𝟙​{h​(x)≠y}−𝟙​{h(t)​(x)≠y})−2​Δ​W\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t)}}\mathds{1}\{h(x)\neq h^{(t)}(x)\}\left(\mathds{1}\{h(x)\neq y\}-\mathds{1}\{h^{(t)}(x)\neq y\}\right)-2\Delta W |  |
|  |  | ≥(1−2​η)​𝐄(x,y)∼D(t)𝟙​{h​(x)≠h(t)​(x)}−2​Δ​W\displaystyle\geq(1-2\eta)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t)}}\mathds{1}\{h(x)\neq h^{(t)}(x)\}-2\Delta W |  |
|  |  | ≥(1−2​η)​𝐄(x,y)∼D(t)𝟙​{h​(x)≠h(T)​(x)}−3​Δ​W.\displaystyle\geq(1-2\eta)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t)}}\mathds{1}\{h(x)\neq h^{(T)}(x)\}-3\Delta W. |  |

This implies that

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄D(t)\[diffj​(h)\]≥(1−2​η)​σj2​(h)−3​W​Δ,\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{D^{(t)}}\[\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)\]\geq(1-2\eta)\sigma\_{j}^{2}(h)-3W\Delta, |  |

which implies that

|     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | V​(h)\displaystyle V(h) | ≤(1−2​η)−1​W−1​∑j=1W𝐄D(t)diffj​(h)+3​(1−2​η)−1​W​Δ\displaystyle\leq(1-2\eta)^{-1}W^{-1}\sum\_{j=1}^{W}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{D^{(t)}}\mathrm{diff}\_{j}(h)+3(1-2\eta)^{-1}W\Delta |  |
|  |  | ≤(1−2​η)−1​(err(T)​(h)−err(T)​(hT))+5​(1−2​η)−1​W​Δ.\displaystyle\leq(1-2\eta)^{-1}\left(\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h)-\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h^{T})\right)+5(1-2\eta)^{-1}W\Delta. |  | (6) |

By ( [B.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A2.Ex37 "Proof of Theorem˜2.1. ‣ B.1 Proof of Theorem˜2.1 ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")), ( [5](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A2.E5 "Equation 5 ‣ Proof of Theorem˜2.1. ‣ B.1 Proof of Theorem˜2.1 ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")) and ( [B.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A2.Ex48 "Proof of Theorem˜2.1. ‣ B.1 Proof of Theorem˜2.1 ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")), we get with probability at least 1−δ1-\delta,

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | err(T)​(h^(i))−err(T)​(hT)≤O~​(d​W−1​(1−2​η)−1+Δ​W)=O~​(d​Δ/(1−2​η)).\displaystyle\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(\hat{h}^{(i)})-\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h^{T})\leq\tilde{O}(dW^{-1}(1-2\eta)^{-1}+\Delta W)=\tilde{O}(\sqrt{d\Delta/(1-2\eta)}). |  |

### B.2 Proof of [Theorem˜2.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S2.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2.2 (Information-Theoretic Lower Bound). ‣ 2 Information-Theoretic Baselines under Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

For convenience, we restate the theorem below.

###### Theorem B.2(Restatement of [Theorem˜2.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S2.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2.2 (Information-Theoretic Lower Bound). ‣ 2 Information-Theoretic Baselines under Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")).

For every T>0T>0 and Δ∈(0,1)\Delta\in(0,1), there is a family of instances of learning halfspaces with η\eta-RCN such that provided (1−2​η)3>d​Δ(1-2\eta)^{3}>d\Delta, there is no learning algorithm 𝒜\mathcal{A} that can achieve error err(T)​(𝒜)≤optT+o​(d​Δ/(1−2​η))\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(\mathcal{A})\leq\mathrm{opt}\_{T}+o(\sqrt{d\Delta/(1-2\eta)}), with probability 1/21/2 for every instance in the family.

Our proof relies on the following simple fact, stated as the following lemma, which is a direct application of \[ [Tsy03](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#bib.bibx40 ""), Theorem 2.2\].

###### Lemma B.1.

Let S=(S1,…,Sd)S=(S\_{1},\dots,S\_{d}) be a random vector drawn uniformly from {±1}\{\pm 1\}. Consider dd coins, such that coin ii has probability pi=1/2+Si​ϵp\_{i}=1/2+S\_{i}\epsilon of landing on head when flipped. Let 𝒜\mathcal{A} be any algorithm that adaptively flips each coin, observes the outcomes and outputs some S^∈{±1}d\hat{S}\in\{\pm 1\}^{d} under the constraints that the number of flips for each coin is at most 1/(100​ϵ2)1/(100\epsilon^{2}) must satisfy 𝐏𝐫(‖S^−S‖1≥d/4)≥1/2\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}(\\|{\hat{S}-S}\\|\_{1}\geq d/4)\geq 1/2.

###### Proof of [Theorem˜2.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S2.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2.2 (Information-Theoretic Lower Bound). ‣ 2 Information-Theoretic Baselines under Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

We construct the following family of sequences of distributions such that each distribution in the sequence corresponds to an instance of learning halfspaces in ℝd\mathbb{R}^{d} with η\eta-RCN. To do this, we first define the marginal distribution DxD\_{x} of the examples as follows. We first sample ii uniformly from \[d\]\[d\] and then sample g∈(0,1)g\in(0,1) uniformly and return x=g​eix=ge\_{i}, where eie\_{i} is the iith standard basis vector.
We next define the family of halfspaces ht(z)h^{(z)}\_{t} for each tt.
For z∈{0,1}dz\in\{0,1\}^{d}, we define ht(z)​(x):=sign​((∑i=1dzi​ei⋅x)−(1−Δ​t​d−1​(1−2​η)−1))h^{(z)}\_{t}(x):=\mathrm{sign}((\sum\_{i=1}^{d}z\_{i}e\_{i}\cdot x)-(1-\Delta td^{-1}(1-2\eta)^{-1})).
That is to say ht(z)​(g​ei)=1h^{(z)}\_{t}(ge\_{i})=1 if and only if zi=1z\_{i}=1 and g≥1−Δ​t​d−1​(1−2​η)−1g\geq 1-\Delta td^{-1}(1-2\eta)^{-1}.

Now we construct the sequence of distributions to learn. Let m=d/(Δ​(1−2​η))/20m=\sqrt{d/(\Delta(1-2\eta))}/20.
For every T>0T>0, we first draw zz from {0,1}d\{0,1\}^{d} uniformly at random and construct distributions D(i)D^{(i)} for i=1,2,…,i=1,2,\dots, based on zz.
If i≤T−mi\leq T-m, let h0(z)h^{(z)}\_{0} be the halfspace that realizes D(i)D^{(i)}
with marginal distribution DxD\_{x}. If i=T−m+ti=T-m+t, where t∈\[m\]t\in\[m\], we let ht(z)h^{(z)}\_{t} be the halfspace that realizes D(i)D^{(i)} with marginal distribution DxD\_{x}.
We notice that for each tt and zz,

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(ht(z)​(x)≠ht+1(z)​(x))≤Δ​(1−2​η)−1.\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D\_{x}}(h^{(z)}\_{t}(x)\neq h^{(z)}\_{t+1}(x))\leq\Delta(1-2\eta)^{-1}. |  |

This implies that for every event A⊆ℝd×{±1}A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{\pm 1\}, we have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | \|𝐏𝐫x∼D(i)(A)−𝐏𝐫x∼D(i+1)(A)\|\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}}(A)-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(i+1)}}(A)\right\| | =\|𝐏𝐫(x,y)∼D(i)(A,ht(z)​(x)≠ht+1(z)​(x))−𝐏𝐫(x,y)∼D(i+1)(A,ht(z)​(x)≠ht+1(z)​(x))\|\displaystyle=\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i)}}(A,h^{(z)}\_{t}(x)\neq h^{(z)}\_{t+1}(x))-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i+1)}}(A,h^{(z)}\_{t}(x)\neq h^{(z)}\_{t+1}(x))\right\| |  |
|  |  | ≤(1−2​η)​Δ​(1−2​η)−1=Δ,\displaystyle\leq(1-2\eta)\Delta(1-2\eta)^{-1}=\Delta, |  |

which implies that dT​V​(D(i),D(i+1))≤Δd\_{\mathrm{T}V}(D^{(i)},D^{(i+1)})\leq\Delta.

For i∈\[d\]i\in\[d\] and j=T−m+tj=T-m+t, we define Xj(i)=𝟙​{x(j)=g​ei,g>(1−Δ​t​d−1​(1−2​η)−1)}X^{(i)}\_{j}=\mathds{1}\{x^{(j)}=ge\_{i},g>(1-\Delta td^{-1}(1-2\eta)^{-1})\}. We have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄​∑t=1m∑i=1dXt(i)≤d​m2​Δ​d−1​(1−2​η)−1≤d/(400​(1−2​η)2),\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\sum\_{t=1}^{m}\sum\_{i=1}^{d}X^{(i)}\_{t}\leq dm^{2}\Delta d^{-1}(1-2\eta)^{-1}\leq d/(400(1-2\eta)^{2}), |  |

where cc is a sufficiently small constant. By Markov’s inequality, this implies that with probability at least 1/21/2, there must be at least d/2d/2 indices ii such that ∑t=1mXt(i)≤1/(100​(1−2​η)2)\sum\_{t=1}^{m}X\_{t}^{(i)}\leq 1/(100(1-2\eta)^{2}). Given this happens, we know from [Lemma˜B.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A2.Thmlemma1 "Lemma B.1. ‣ B.2 Proof of Theorem˜2.2 ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") that any algorithm 𝒜′\mathcal{A}^{\prime} fails to predict ziz\_{i} for d/8d/8 of the indices with probability at least 1/21/2.

We now show that any algorithm 𝒜\mathcal{A} that outputs a hypothesis hh with error err(T)​(h)≤η+d​Δ/(1−2​η)/100\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h)\leq\eta+\sqrt{d\Delta/(1-2\eta)}/100, gives an algorithm that correctly predicts ziz\_{i}. Since

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | err(T)​(h)=η+𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(h​(x)≠hm(z)​(x))​(1−2​η),\displaystyle\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(h)=\eta+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D\_{x}}(h(x)\neq h^{(z)}\_{m}(x))(1-2\eta), |  |

we know that 𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(h​(x)≠hm(z)​(x))≤(1−2​η)−1​d​Δ/(1−2​η)/100\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D\_{x}}(h(x)\neq h^{(z)}\_{m}(x))\leq(1-2\eta)^{-1}\sqrt{d\Delta/(1-2\eta)}/100. This implies that for those d/2d/2 indices ii such that ∑t=1mXt(i)≤1/(100​(1−2​η)2)\sum\_{t=1}^{m}X\_{t}^{(i)}\leq 1/(100(1-2\eta)^{2}), there must be at least 3​d/83d/8 of the indices ii such that

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(h​(x)=hm(z)​(x)​∣x=g​ei,g>​1−(1−2​η)−1​d​Δ/(1−2​η))>1/2.\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D\_{x}}(h(x)=h^{(z)}\_{m}(x)\mid x=ge\_{i},g>1-(1-2\eta)^{-1}\sqrt{d\Delta/(1-2\eta)})>1/2. |  |

f
Now we define 𝒜′\mathcal{A}^{\prime} by predicting ziz\_{i} as the majority of hm(z)​(x)h^{(z)}\_{m}(x) given gei,g>1−(1−2η)−1d​Δ/(1−2​η))ge\_{i},g>1-(1-2\eta)^{-1}\sqrt{d\Delta/(1-2\eta)}). This implies that 𝒜′\mathcal{A}^{\prime} must succeed in predicting 3​d/83d/8 of the ziz\_{i}s, yielding a contradiction.
∎

### B.3 Statistical Excess Error for Learning Drifting γ\gamma-Margin Halfspaces

###### Theorem B.3.

For every T>0T>0 and Δ∈(0,1)\Delta\in(0,1), there is a family of instances of learning γ\gamma-margin halfspaces with η\eta-RCN such that provided (1−2​η)2>d​Δ(1-2\eta)^{2}>d\Delta, there is no learning algorithm 𝒜\mathcal{A} that can achieve error err(T)​(𝒜)≤optT+o​(Δ/γ)\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(\mathcal{A})\leq\mathrm{opt}\_{T}+o(\sqrt{\Delta}/\gamma), with probability 1/21/2 for every instance in the family.

###### Proof of [Theorem˜B.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A2.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem B.3. ‣ B.3 Statistical Excess Error for Learning Drifting 𝛾-Margin Halfspaces ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

For γ∈(0,1)\gamma\in(0,1) small enough, we choose d=Θ​(1/γ2)d=\Theta(1/\gamma^{2}).
Define x(0)=ed+1−∑i=1deix^{(0)}=e\_{d+1}-\sum\_{i=1}^{d}e\_{i} and x(i)=ei−(ed+1/2)∈ℝd+1x^{(i)}=e\_{i}-(e\_{d+1}/2)\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}. For z∈{0,1}dz\in\{0,1\}^{d}, we define halfspace h(z)​(x)=sign​(w(z)⋅x),w(z)=(−ed+1+∑i=1dzi​ei)h^{(z)}(x)=\mathrm{sign}(w^{(z)}\cdot x),w^{(z)}=(-e\_{d+1}+\sum\_{i=1}^{d}z\_{i}e\_{i}). We first show that any halfspace h(z)​(x)h^{(z)}(x) has γ\gamma-margin with any
marginal distribution over X={x(i)}i=0dX=\{x^{(i)}\}\_{i=0}^{d}.
For x(0)x^{(0)}, we have (−ed+1+∑i=1dzi​ei)⋅x(0)=−‖z‖1−1(-e\_{d+1}+\sum\_{i=1}^{d}z\_{i}e\_{i})\cdot x^{(0)}=-\left\\|z\right\\|\_{1}-1, which implies

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | \|w(z)⋅x(0)\|‖w(z)‖2​‖x(0)‖2=‖z‖1+1‖w(z)‖2​‖x(0)‖2≥‖z‖1+1d=Ω​(γ).\displaystyle\frac{\left\|w^{(z)}\cdot x^{(0)}\right\|}{\left\\|w^{(z)}\right\\|\_{2}\left\\|x^{(0)}\right\\|\_{2}}=\frac{\left\\|z\right\\|\_{1}+1}{\left\\|w^{(z)}\right\\|\_{2}\left\\|x^{(0)}\right\\|\_{2}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{\left\\|z\right\\|\_{1}+1}{d}}=\Omega(\gamma). |  |

For x(i)x^{(i)}, we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | \|w(z)⋅x(i)\|‖w(z)‖2​‖x(0)‖2≥1/2‖w(z)‖2​‖x(0)‖2≥1/2d+1=Ω​(1/γ).\displaystyle\frac{\left\|w^{(z)}\cdot x^{(i)}\right\|}{\left\\|w^{(z)}\right\\|\_{2}\left\\|x^{(0)}\right\\|\_{2}}\geq\frac{1/2}{\left\\|w^{(z)}\right\\|\_{2}\left\\|x^{(0)}\right\\|\_{2}}\geq\frac{1/2}{\sqrt{d+1}}=\Omega(1/\gamma). |  |

We next design the family of sequences of drifted distributions D(1),…,D(T)D^{(1)},\dots,D^{(T)}. For any z∈{0,1}dz\in\{0,1\}^{d}, we fix the target halfspace to be h(z)h^{(z)}. Choose m=d/Δ​(1−2​η)−1m=\sqrt{d/\Delta}(1-2\eta)^{-1}. For t≤T−mt\leq T-m, we define the marginal distribution (Dt)x(D\_{t})\_{x} to be the Dirac function at point x(0)x^{(0)}. For t=T−m+jt=T-m+j, j∈\[m\]j\in\[m\], choose (D(t))x=(1−pt)​δ​(x(0))+∑i=1d(pt/d)​δ​(x(i))(D^{(t)})\_{x}=(1-p\_{t})\delta(x^{(0)})+\sum\_{i=1}^{d}(p\_{t}/d)\delta(x^{(i)}), where pt=Δ​jp\_{t}=\Delta j.
We notice that dT​V​(Dt,Dt+1)≤Δd\_{\mathrm{T}V}(D\_{t},D\_{t+1})\leq\Delta.

Let z∼{0,1}dz\sim\{0,1\}^{d} be chosen uniformly at random, we will show that no algorithm in expectation can achieve O​(d​Δ)O(\sqrt{d\Delta}) error. By the choice of zz, we know that h(z)​(x(i))=1h^{(z)}(x^{(i)})=1 with probability 1/21/2 for every i∈\[d\]i\in\[d\]. By the design of DtD\_{t}, we know that Dx(t)​(x(i))≤Dx(T)​(x(i))=Δ/d​(1−2​η)−1D^{(t)}\_{x}(x^{(i)})\leq D^{(T)}\_{x}(x^{(i)})=\sqrt{\Delta/d}(1-2\eta)^{-1}.
Let Xt(i)=𝟙​{xt=x(i)}X^{(i)}\_{t}=\mathds{1}\{x\_{t}=x^{(i)}\}, xt∼Dx(t)x\_{t}\sim D^{(t)}\_{x}
This implies

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄​∑t=1T∑i=1dXt(i)=𝐄​∑t=T−mT∑i=1dXt(i)≤d​m​Δ/d​(1−2​η)−1≤c​d/(1−2​η)2\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\sum\_{t=1}^{T}\sum\_{i=1}^{d}X^{(i)}\_{t}=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\sum\_{t=T-m}^{T}\sum\_{i=1}^{d}X^{(i)}\_{t}\leq dm\sqrt{\Delta/d}(1-2\eta)^{-1}\leq cd/(1-2\eta)^{2} |  |

for some c<1/100c<1/100.
This implies that there must be d/2d/2, i∈\[d\]i\in\[d\] such that ∑t=1TXt(i)≤c/(1−2​η)2\sum\_{t=1}^{T}X^{(i)}\_{t}\leq c/(1-2\eta)^{2}. Since the distribution of the label of x(i)x^{(i)} is independent of the marginal distribution, by [Lemma˜B.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A2.Thmlemma1 "Lemma B.1. ‣ B.2 Proof of Theorem˜2.2 ‣ Appendix B Omitted Proofs from Section˜2 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"),
we know that for these x(i)x^{(i)}, we cannot tell if the ground truth label is +1+1 or −1-1, which implies that the prediction error is at least Ω​(d​Δ)\Omega(\sqrt{d\Delta}).
∎

### C.2 Omitted Proofs for [Lemma˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 3.1 (Regret to Error). ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

We restate the lemma below.

###### Lemma C.1(Restatement of [Lemma˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 3.1 (Regret to Error). ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")).

Let i,m∈ℕi,m\in\mathbb{N} and let w∗∈𝕊d−1w^{\*}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} be a vector that realizes D(i+m)D^{(i+m)}. There exists a function Fi:𝕊d−1×𝕊d−1×𝕊d−1→ℝF\_{i}:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R} such that
for any x∈𝕊d−1x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} and for any w∈𝕊d−1w\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅(w−w∗)≥2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η)−Fi​(w∗,w,x).\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot(w-w^{\*})\;\geq 2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta)-F\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x). |  |

Additionally, \|Fi\|≤1/γ\left\|F\_{i}\right\|\leq 1/\gamma, and for every w∈𝕊d−1w\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}, 𝐄x∼Dx(i)Fi​(w∗,w,x)≤O​(Δ​m/γ)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}F\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\leq O(\Delta m/\gamma).

###### Proof of [Lemma˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 3.1 (Regret to Error). ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

The proof of [Lemma˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 3.1 (Regret to Error). ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") is divided into two cases based on \|w⋅x\|\left\|w\cdot x\right\|.

##### Case 1: Large Margin

In the first case, \|w⋅x\|≥γ\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma and we establish the following claim.

###### Claim C.1.

Let i,m∈ℕi,m\in\mathbb{N} and let (w∗)(i)∈𝕊d−1(w^{\*})^{(i)}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} be a vector that realizes D(i)D^{(i)}. Denote by fi​(x,w∗,x)=((1−2​ηm+i​(x))−(1−2​ηi​(x))​ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x))​\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|f\_{i}(x,w^{\*},x)=\left((1-2\eta\_{m+i}(x))-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\|. Then, for every x∈𝕊d−1x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} such that \|w⋅x\|≥γ\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma, 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅(w−w∗)≥2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η)−fi​(w∗,w,x)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot(w-w^{\*})\geq 2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta)-f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x).

We defer the proof of [˜C.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim1 "Claim C.1. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") to [Section˜C.2.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS1 "C.2.1 Proof of ˜C.1 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").
Since \|w⋅x\|≥γ\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma, we know that \|fi​(w∗,w,x)\|≤1/γ\left\|f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\right\|\leq 1/\gamma. We next upper bound the expectation of fi​(w∗,w,x)f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x). Formally, we have the following claim, whose proof is deferred to [Section˜C.2.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS2 "C.2.2 Proof of ˜C.2 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

###### Claim C.2.

For every w∈𝕊d−1w\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}, it holds 𝐄(x,y)∼D(i)fi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|≥γ}=O​(m​Δ/γ).\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i)}}f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\}=O(m\Delta/\gamma).

To prove [˜C.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim2 "Claim C.2. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), we establish the following two claims that will be used frequently. We defer the proofs of [˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S3.Thmclaim1 "Claim 3.1. ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and [˜3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S3.Thmclaim2 "Claim 3.2. ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") to [Section˜C.2.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS3 "C.2.3 Proof of ˜3.1 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and [Section˜C.2.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS4 "C.2.4 Proof of ˜3.2 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

##### Case 2: Small Margin

We next consider the case where \|w⋅x\|<γ\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma.
We will discuss two cases. In the first case, we have sign​(w⋅x)=sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)=\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x), which implies that err(i)​(w,x)−η=ηi​(x)−η≤0\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta=\eta\_{i}(x)-\eta\leq 0.
In this case, we establish the following claim.

###### Claim C.3.

Let i,m∈ℕi,m\in\mathbb{N} and let (w∗)(i)∈𝕊d−1(w^{\*})^{(i)}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} be a vector that realizes D(i)D^{(i)}. Denote by Gi​(w∗,w,x)=((1−2​ηm+i​(x))−(1−2​ηi​(x))​ϕ​((w∗)(i)⋅x,w∗⋅x))​\|w∗⋅x\|/γG\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)=\left((1-2\eta\_{m+i}(x))-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\phi((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x,w^{\*}\cdot x)\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\gamma, then for every x∈𝕊d−1x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} such that \|w⋅x\|<γ\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma and sign​(w⋅x)=sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)=\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x), we have 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅(w−w∗)≥2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η)−Gi​(w∗,w,x)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot(w-w^{\*})\geq 2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta)-G\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x).

Notice that \|Gi​(w∗,w,x)\|≤1/γ\left\|G\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\right\|\leq 1/\gamma always holds. We next upper bound the expectation of Gi​(w∗,w,x)G\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x) with the following claim.

###### Claim C.4.

For every w∈𝕊d−1w\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}, it holds 𝐄(x,y)∼D(i)Gi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|<γ,sign​(w⋅x)=sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)}=O​(m​Δ/γ).\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i)}}G\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma,\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)=\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\}=O(m\Delta/\gamma).

We defer the proof of [˜C.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim3 "Claim C.3. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and [˜C.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim4 "Claim C.4. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") to [Section˜C.2.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS5 "C.2.5 Proof of ˜C.3 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and [Section˜C.2.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS6 "C.2.6 Proof of ˜C.4 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

Next, we consider the case, where sign​(w⋅x)≠sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)\neq\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x), which implies that err(i)​(w,x)−η=1−ηi​(x)−η≥0\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta=1-\eta\_{i}(x)-\eta\geq 0. We give the following claim in this case.

###### Claim C.5.

Let i,m∈ℕi,m\in\mathbb{N} and let (w∗)(i)∈𝕊d−1(w^{\*})^{(i)}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} be a vector that realizes D(i)D^{(i)} and w∗∈𝕊d−1w^{\*}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} be a vector that realizes D(i+m)D^{(i+m)} . Denote by

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | Vi​(w∗,x)\displaystyle V\_{i}(w^{\*},x) | =(1−η−ηi​(x))​𝟙​{ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=1,\|w∗⋅x\|<γ}​(γ−\|w∗⋅x\|)/γ,\displaystyle=(1-\eta-\eta\_{i}(x))\mathds{1}\{\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=1,\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|<\gamma\}(\gamma-\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|)/\gamma, |  |
|  | Ui​(w∗,x)\displaystyle U\_{i}(w^{\*},x) | =(1−η−ηi​(x))​𝟙​{ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1}​(\|w∗⋅x\|+γ)/γ.\displaystyle=(1-\eta-\eta\_{i}(x))\mathds{1}\{\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1\}(\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|+\gamma)/\gamma. |  |

Then for every x∈𝕊d−1x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} such that \|w⋅x\|<γ\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma and sign​(w⋅x)≠sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)\neq\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x), we have 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅(w−w∗)≥2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η)−Vi​(w∗,x)−Ui​(w∗,x)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot(w-w^{\*})\geq 2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta)-V\_{i}(w^{\*},x)-U\_{i}(w^{\*},x).

Since \|Vi\|,\|Ui\|=O​(1/γ)\left\|V\_{i}\right\|,\left\|U\_{i}\right\|=O(1/\gamma) always hold, we thus upper bound the expectation of ViV\_{i} and UiU\_{i}. We make the following claim.

###### Claim C.6.

Let i,m∈ℕi,m\in\mathbb{N} and let (w∗)(i)∈𝕊d−1(w^{\*})^{(i)}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} be a vector that realizes D(i)D^{(i)} and w∗∈𝕊d−1w^{\*}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} be a vector that realizes D(i+m)D^{(i+m)}. Then

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(i)Vi​(w∗,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|<γ,sign​(w⋅x)≠sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)}=O​(m​Δ)\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i)}}V\_{i}(w^{\*},x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma,\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)\neq\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\}=O(m\Delta) |  |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(i)Ui​(w∗,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|<γ,sign​(w⋅x)≠sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)}=O​(m​Δ)\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i)}}U\_{i}(w^{\*},x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma,\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)\neq\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\}=O(m\Delta) |  |

We defer the proof of [˜C.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim5 "Claim C.5. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and [˜C.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim6 "Claim C.6. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") to [Section˜C.2.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS7 "C.2.7 Proof of ˜C.5 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and [Section˜C.2.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.SS2.SSS8 "C.2.8 Proof of ˜C.6 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and conclude the proof of [Lemma˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 3.1 (Regret to Error). ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") using the claims we have established so far. Define

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | Fi​(w∗,w,x)\displaystyle F\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x) | :=fi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|≥γ}+Gi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|<γ,sign​(w⋅x)=sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)}\displaystyle:=f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\}+G\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma,\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)=\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\} |  |
|  |  | +(Ui​(w∗,x)+Vi​(w∗,x))​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|<γ,sign​(w⋅x)≠sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)}.\displaystyle+(U\_{i}(w^{\*},x)+V\_{i}(w^{\*},x))\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma,\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)\neq\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\}. |  |

By [˜C.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim1 "Claim C.1. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), [˜C.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim3 "Claim C.3. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), and [˜C.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim5 "Claim C.5. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), we know that for every w∈𝕊d−1w\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1} and for every x∈𝕊d−1x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}, we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅(w−w∗)≥2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η)−Fi​(w∗,w,x).\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot(w-w^{\*})\geq 2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta)-F\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x). |  |

Since \|fi\|,\|Gi\|,\|Ui\|,\|Vi\|=O​(1/γ)\left\|f\_{i}\right\|,\left\|G\_{i}\right\|,\left\|U\_{i}\right\|,\left\|V\_{i}\right\|=O(1/\gamma), we know that \|Fi\|=O​(1/γ)\left\|F\_{i}\right\|=O(1/\gamma). Furthermore, by [˜C.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim2 "Claim C.2. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), [˜C.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim4 "Claim C.4. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), and [˜C.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Thmclaim6 "Claim C.6. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), we have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄x∼Dx(i)Fi​(w∗,w,x)\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}F\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x) | =𝐄x∼Dx(i)fi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|≥γ}\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\} |  |
|  |  | +𝐄x∼Dx(i)Gi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|<γ,sign​(w⋅x)=sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)}\displaystyle+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}G\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma,\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)=\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\} |  |
|  |  | +𝐄x∼Dx(i)(Ui​(w∗,x)+Vi​(w∗,x))⁡𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|<γ,sign​(w⋅x)≠sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)}\displaystyle+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}(U\_{i}(w^{\*},x)+V\_{i}(w^{\*},x))\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|<\gamma,\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)\neq\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\} |  |
|  |  | =O​(m​Δ/γ).\displaystyle=O(m\Delta/\gamma). |  |

∎

#### C.2.1 Proof of [˜C.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A3.Thmclaim1 "Claim C.1. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

###### Proof of [˜C.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A3.Thmclaim1 "Claim C.1. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

We start by bounding 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅w\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot w.

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅w=\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot w= | (((1−2​η)​sign​(w⋅x)−𝐄y​(x))​x/max⁡{\|w⋅x\|,γ})⋅w\displaystyle\left(((1-2\eta)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}y(x))x/\max\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|,\gamma\}\right)\cdot w |  |
|  | =\displaystyle= | sign​(w⋅x)​((1−2​η)​sign​(w⋅x)−(1−2​ηi​(x))​sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x))\displaystyle\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)\left((1-2\eta)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\right) |  |
|  | =\displaystyle= | (1−2​η)−(1−2​ηi​(x))​sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)​sign​(w⋅x)\displaystyle(1-2\eta)-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\mathrm{sign}\left((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x\right)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x) |  |
|  | =\displaystyle= | (1−2​η)−(1−2​ηi​(x))​ϕ​((w∗)(i)⋅x,w⋅x).\displaystyle(1-2\eta)-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\phi\left((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x,w\cdot x\right). |  |

We consider two cases. If sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)=sign​(w⋅x)\mathrm{sign}\left((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x\right)=\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x), which implies ϕ​((w∗)(i)⋅x,w⋅x)=1\phi\left((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x,w\cdot x\right)=1,
then we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅w=2​(ηi​(x)−η)=2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η).\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot w=2(\eta\_{i}(x)-\eta)=2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta). |  |

If sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x)≠sign​(w⋅x)\mathrm{sign}\left((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x\right)\neq\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x), which implies ϕ​((w∗)(i)⋅x,w⋅x)=−1\phi\left((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x,w\cdot x\right)=-1
then we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅w=2​(1−ηi​(x)−η)=2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η).\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot w=2(1-\eta\_{i}(x)-\eta)=2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta). |  |

Thus, it always holds that

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅w=2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η).\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot w=2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta). |  | (8) |

It remains to analyze the term with respect to w∗w^{\*}. We have

|     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅w∗=\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot w^{\*}= | (((1−2​η)​sign​(w⋅x)−𝐄y​(x))​x/\|w⋅x\|)⋅w∗\displaystyle\left(((1-2\eta)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}y(x))x/\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\right)\cdot w^{\*} |  |
|  | =\displaystyle= | sign​(w∗⋅x)​((1−2​η)​sign​(w⋅x)−(1−2​ηi​(x))​sign​((w∗)(i)⋅x))​\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|\displaystyle\mathrm{sign}(w^{\*}\cdot x)\left((1-2\eta)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\mathrm{sign}((w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\| |  |
|  | =\displaystyle= | ((1−2​η)​ϕ​(w⋅x,w∗⋅x)−(1−2​ηm+i​(x)))​\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|\displaystyle\left((1-2\eta)\phi(w\cdot x,w^{\*}\cdot x)-(1-2\eta\_{m+i}(x))\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\| |  |
|  | +\displaystyle+ | ((1−2​ηm+i​(x))−(1−2​ηi​(x))​ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x))​\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|\displaystyle\left((1-2\eta\_{m+i}(x))-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\| |  |
|  |  | ≤((1−2​ηm+i​(x))−(1−2​ηi​(x))​ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x))​\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|.\displaystyle\leq\left((1-2\eta\_{m+i}(x))-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\|. |  | (9) |

Here, the inequality holds because (1−2​η)​ϕ​(w⋅x,w∗⋅x)≤(1−2​η)≤(1−2​ηi​(x))(1-2\eta)\phi(w\cdot x,w^{\*}\cdot x)\leq(1-2\eta)\leq(1-2\eta\_{i}(x)). Combine ( [8](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.E8 "Equation 8 ‣ Proof of ˜C.1. ‣ C.2.1 Proof of ˜C.1 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")) and ( [C.2.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Ex84 "Proof of ˜C.1. ‣ C.2.1 Proof of ˜C.1 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")), we have 𝐄y∼D(i)∣xg​(w;x,y)⋅(w−w∗)≥2​(err(i)​(w,x)−η)−fi​(w∗,w,x)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{y\sim D^{(i)}\mid x}g(w;x,y)\cdot(w-w^{\*})\geq 2(\mathrm{err}^{(i)}(w,x)-\eta)-f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x) since \|w⋅x\|≥γ\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma.
∎

#### C.2.2 Proof of [˜C.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A3.Thmclaim2 "Claim C.2. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

###### Proof of [˜C.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A3.Thmclaim2 "Claim C.2. ‣ Case 1: Large Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

By taking the expectation over x∼Dx(i)x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}, we have

|     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  |  | 𝐄x∼D(i)xfi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|≥γ}\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)\_{x}}}f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\} |  |
|  | =\displaystyle= | 𝐄x∼Dx(i)((1−2​ηm+i​(x))−(1−2​ηi​(x))​ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x))⁡\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|≥γ}\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}\left((1-2\eta\_{m+i}(x))-(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\} |  |
|  | =\displaystyle= | 𝐄x∼Dx(i)((1−2​ηm+i​(x))+(1−2​ηi​(x)))⁡\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|​𝟙​{ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1,\|w⋅x\|≥γ}\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}\left((1-2\eta\_{m+i}(x))+(1-2\eta\_{i}(x))\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\mathds{1}\{\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1,\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\} |  |
|  | +\displaystyle+ | 𝐄x∼Dx(i)(2​ηi​(x)−2​ηm+i​(x))⁡\|w∗⋅x\|/\|w⋅x\|​𝟙​{ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=1,\|w⋅x\|≥γ}\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}\left(2\eta\_{i}(x)-2\eta\_{m+i}(x)\right)\left\|w^{\*}\cdot x\right\|/\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\mathds{1}\{\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=1,\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\} |  |
|  | ≤\displaystyle\leq | 2​𝐏𝐫x∼Dz(i)(ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1)​(1−2​η)/γ\displaystyle\;2\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{z}}(\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1)(1-2\eta)/\gamma |  |
|  | +\displaystyle+ | 2​𝐄x∼Dx(i)(ηi​(x)−ηm+i​(x))⁡𝟙​{ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=1,ηi​(x)≥ηm+i​(x)}/γ\displaystyle\;2\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}\left(\eta\_{i}(x)-\eta\_{m+i}(x)\right)\mathds{1}\{\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=1,\eta\_{i}(x)\geq\eta\_{m+i}(x)\}/\gamma |  | (10) |

By [˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S3.Thmclaim1 "Claim 3.1. ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and [˜3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S3.Thmclaim2 "Claim 3.2. ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), we know that 𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(i)(ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1)≤O​(Δ​m/(1−2​η))\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}(\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1)\leq O(\Delta m/(1-2\eta)) and 𝐄x∼Dx(i)(ηi​(x)−ηm+i​(x))⁡𝟙​{ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=1,ηi​(x)≥ηm+i​(x)}≤O​(Δ​m)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}\left(\eta\_{i}(x)-\eta\_{m+i}(x)\right)\mathds{1}\{\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=1,\eta\_{i}(x)\geq\eta\_{m+i}(x)\}\leq O(\Delta m). Thus, by( [C.2.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.Ex88 "Proof of ˜C.2. ‣ C.2.2 Proof of ˜C.2 ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")), we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(i)fi​(w∗,w,x)​𝟙​{\|w⋅x\|≥γ}≤O​(m​Δ/γ).\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i)}}f\_{i}(w^{\*},w,x)\mathds{1}\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|\geq\gamma\}\leq O(m\Delta/\gamma). |  |

∎

#### C.2.3 Proof of [˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmclaim1 "Claim 3.1. ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

###### Proof of [˜3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmclaim1 "Claim 3.1. ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

Suppose that
𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(i)(ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1)≥(1+η)​Δ​m/(1−2​η)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}(\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1)\geq(1+\eta)\Delta m/(1-2\eta), then
we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | (𝐏𝐫(x,y)∼D(i)−𝐏𝐫(x,y)∼D(m+i))​(ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1,sign​(w∗⋅x)≠y)\displaystyle\left(\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(i)}}-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(m+i)}}\right)\left(\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1,\mathrm{sign}(w^{\*}\cdot x)\neq y\right) |  |
|  | ≥(1−η)​𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(i)(ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1)−η​𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(m+1)(ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1)\displaystyle\geq(1-\eta)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}(\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1)-\eta\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(m+1)}\_{x}}(\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1) |  |
|  | >(1−η)​(1+η)​Δ​m/(1−2​η)−η​(1+η)​Δ​m/(1−2​η)−η​Δ​m=Δ​m,\displaystyle>(1-\eta)(1+\eta)\Delta m/(1-2\eta)-\eta(1+\eta)\Delta m/(1-2\eta)-\eta\Delta m=\Delta m, |  |

which contradicts with the fact that dT​V​(D(i),D(j))≤m​Δd\_{\mathrm{T}V}(D^{(i)},D^{(j)})\leq m\Delta. This implies 𝐏𝐫x∼Dx(i)(ϕ​(w∗⋅x,(w∗)(i)⋅x)=−1)≤O​(Δ​m/(1−2​η))\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{x\sim D^{(i)}\_{x}}(\phi(w^{\*}\cdot x,(w^{\*})^{(i)}\cdot x)=-1)\leq O(\Delta m/(1-2\eta)).
∎

#### C.2.5 Proof of [˜C.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A3.Thmclaim3 "Claim C.3. ‣ Case 2: Small Margin ‣ C.2 Omitted Proofs for Lemma˜3.1 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

we have

|  | =\displaystyle= | (((1−2​η)​sign​(w⋅x)−𝐄y​(x))​x/max⁡{\|w⋅x\|,γ})⋅(w−w∗)\displaystyle\left(((1-2\eta)\mathrm{sign}(w\cdot x)-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}y(x))x/\max\{\left\|w\cdot x\right\|,\gamma\}\right)\cdot(w-w^{\*}) |  |

### C.5 Proof of [Theorem˜3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 3.2 (Efficient Drift Learning in Realizable Case). ‣ Efficiently Learning Realizable Drifting Halfspaces ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

We restate the theorem below.

###### Theorem C.2(Restatement of [Theorem˜3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 3.2 (Efficient Drift Learning in Realizable Case). ‣ Efficiently Learning Realizable Drifting Halfspaces ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")).

Consider the problem of learning drifting halfspaces. There is an algorithm 𝒜\mathcal{A} such that for any time step T=Ω~​((γ​Δ)−1/2)T=\tilde{\Omega}((\gamma\Delta)^{-1/2}), 𝒜\mathcal{A} runs in poly​(d,1/γ,Δ)\mathrm{poly}(d,1/\gamma,\Delta) time and outputs a hypothesis h^(T)\hat{h}^{(T)} such that with probability at least 9/109/10, err(T)​(h^(T))=O~​(Δ​γ−3/2)\mathrm{err}^{(T)}(\hat{h}^{(T)})=\tilde{O}(\sqrt{\Delta}\gamma^{-3/2}).

Algorithm 5DriftedHalfspace (Learning Drifting Realizable Halfspaces)

1:Input:γ∈(0,1):\gamma\in(0,1): margin parameter, Δ>0\Delta>0 drift rate

2:Output: Predicted label y^(t)∈{±1}\hat{y}^{(t)}\in\{\pm 1\} for x(t)x^{(t)}, t≥1t\geq 1

3:Let W=Θ~​((γ​Δ)−1/2),i=1W=\tilde{\Theta}((\gamma\Delta)^{-1/2}),i=1, h^0​(x)≡1\hat{h}^{0}(x)\equiv 1

4:fort=1,2,…t=1,2,\dotsdo

5:  Output hypothesis h(i−1)h^{(i-1)} and receive (x(t),y(t))(x^{(t)},y^{(t)})

6:S(i)←S(i)∪{(x(t),y(t))}S^{(i)}\leftarrow S^{(i)}\cup\{(x^{(t)},y^{(t)})\}

7:ift=i​Wt=iWthen

8:h(i)←RealizablePerceptron​(γ,S(i),γ)h^{(i)}\leftarrow\textsc{RealizablePerceptron}(\gamma,S^{(i)},\gamma), S(i)←∅S^{(i)}\leftarrow\emptyset

9:i←i+1i\leftarrow i+1

Algorithm 6RealizablePerceptron (Subrountine for Learning a Single Halfspace over a Dataset)

1:Input:γ∈(0,1):\gamma\in(0,1): margin parameter,
S={(x(i),y(i))}i=1mS=\{(x^{(i)},y^{(i)})\}\_{i=1}^{m} a dataset of 2​m2m labeled examples,μ>0\mu>0 step size,

2:Output:h^=sign​(w^⋅x):ℝd→{±1}\hat{h}=\mathrm{sign}(\hat{w}\cdot x):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\{\pm 1\}

3:w(0)=e1w^{(0)}=e\_{1}

4:Split S=S1∪S2S=S\_{1}\cup S\_{2}, where S1={(x(i),y(i))}i=1m/2,S2=S∖S1S\_{1}=\{(x^{(i)},y^{(i)})\}\_{i=1}^{m/2},S\_{2}=S\setminus S\_{1}

5:fori=1,2,…,mi=1,2,\dots,mdo

6:g​(w(i),x,y)←(sign​(w(i)⋅x)−y)​xg(w^{(i)},x,y)\leftarrow(\mathrm{sign}(w^{(i)}\cdot x)-y)x

7:w(i+1)=proj𝔹​(1)​(w(i)−μ​gt​(w(i),x,y))w^{(i+1)}=\mathrm{proj}\_{\mathbb{B}(1)}(w^{(i)}-\mu g\_{t}(w^{(i)},x,y)), h^i+1​(x)=sign​(w(i+1)⋅x)\hat{h}\_{i+1}(x)=\mathrm{sign}(w^{(i+1)}\cdot x)

8:Return h^=argminh^ierr^​(h^i)\hat{h}=\operatorname\*{argmin}\_{\hat{h}\_{i}}\hat{\mathrm{err}}(\hat{h}\_{i}), where err^​(h^i)=1\|S2\|​∑(x,y)∈S2𝟙​{h^i​(x)≠y}\hat{\mathrm{err}}(\hat{h}\_{i})=\frac{1}{\|S\_{2}\|}\sum\_{(x,y)\in S\_{2}}\mathds{1}\{\hat{h}\_{i}(x)\neq y\}.

###### Proof of [Theorem˜3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 3.2 (Efficient Drift Learning in Realizable Case). ‣ Efficiently Learning Realizable Drifting Halfspaces ‣ 3 Efficiently Learning Drifted Halfspaces with Massart Noise ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

We will show that for t=i​Wt=iW, err(t)​(h(i))≤O~​(Δ1/2/γ3/2)\mathrm{err}^{(t)}(h^{(i)})\leq\tilde{O}(\Delta^{1/2}/\gamma^{3/2}).
Suppose this is correct, then for t=i​W+jt=iW+j, j≤Wj\leq W, we have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | err(t)​(h(i))\displaystyle\mathrm{err}^{(t)}(h^{(i)}) | =𝐏𝐫(x,y)∼D(t)(h(i)​(x)≠y)≤𝐏𝐫(x,y)∼D(i​W)(h(i)​(x)≠y)+j​Δ\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t)}}(h^{(i)}(x)\neq y)\leq\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(iW)}}(h^{(i)}(x)\neq y)+j\Delta |  |
|  |  | ≤O~​(Δ1/2/γ3/2)+Δ1/2/γ=O~​(Δ1/2/γ3/2).\displaystyle\leq\tilde{O}(\Delta^{1/2}/\gamma^{3/2})+\Delta^{1/2}/\gamma=\tilde{O}(\Delta^{1/2}/\gamma^{3/2}). |  |

To do this, we will analyze [Algorithm˜6](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#alg6 "In C.5 Proof of Theorem˜3.2 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") with t=i​Wt=iW. Let w(i)w^{(i)} be the vector in the iith round of update in [Algorithm˜6](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#alg6 "In C.5 Proof of Theorem˜3.2 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") and let w∗w^{\*} be the unit vector that realizes D(t+T)D^{(t+T)}, where T=W/2T=W/2.
By the contraction of the projection operator, we have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | ‖w(i+1)−w∗‖2\displaystyle\left\\|w^{(i+1)}-w^{\*}\right\\|^{2} | ≤‖w(i)−w∗−μ​g​(w(i);x,y)‖2\displaystyle\leq\left\\|w^{(i)}-w^{\*}-\mu g(w^{(i)};x,y)\right\\|^{2} |  |
|  |  | ≤‖w(i)−w∗‖2+μ2​‖g​(w(i);x,y)‖2−2​μ​g​(w(i);x,y)⋅(w(i)−w∗)\displaystyle\leq\left\\|w^{(i)}-w^{\*}\right\\|^{2}+\mu^{2}\left\\|g(w^{(i)};x,y)\right\\|^{2}-2\mu g(w^{(i)};x,y)\cdot(w^{(i)}-w^{\*}) |  |
|  |  | =‖w(i)−w∗‖2+μ2​𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}+2​μ​𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​y​x⋅(w(i)−w∗)\displaystyle=\left\\|w^{(i)}-w^{\*}\right\\|^{2}+\mu^{2}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}+2\mu\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}yx\cdot(w^{(i)}-w^{\*}) |  |

Rearrange the inequality we obtain that

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​(2​μ​y​x⋅(w∗−w(i))−μ2)≤‖w(i)−w∗‖2−‖w(i+1)−w∗‖2,\displaystyle\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot(w^{\*}-w^{(i)})-\mu^{2}\right)\leq\left\\|w^{(i)}-w^{\*}\right\\|^{2}-\left\\|w^{(i+1)}-w^{\*}\right\\|^{2}, |  |

which implies

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)≤‖w(i)−w∗‖2−‖w(i+1)−w∗‖2\displaystyle\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right)\leq\left\\|w^{(i)}-w^{\*}\right\\|^{2}-\left\\|w^{(i+1)}-w^{\*}\right\\|^{2} |  |

Denote by Ii:=𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)I\_{i}:=\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right) for i∈\[T\]i\in\[T\].
We notice that for every w(i)∈𝕊d−1w^{(i)}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I\_{i} | =𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗≥γ}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}\geq\gamma\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right) |  |
|  |  | +𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗<γ}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)\displaystyle+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}<\gamma\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right) |  |
|  |  | ≥𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗≥γ}​μ​(2​γ−μ)\displaystyle\geq\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}\geq\gamma\}\mu(2\gamma-\mu) |  |
|  |  | +𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗<γ}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)\displaystyle+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}<\gamma\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right) |  |
|  |  | =𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​μ​(2​γ−μ)\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mu(2\gamma-\mu) |  |
|  |  | −𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗<γ}​μ​(2​γ−μ)\displaystyle-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}<\gamma\}\mu(2\gamma-\mu) |  |
|  |  | +𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗<γ}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)\displaystyle+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}<\gamma\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right) |  |

We upper bound the three terms separately. For the first term, we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​μ​(2​γ−μ)≥μ​γ​err(t+i)​(w(i)),\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mu(2\gamma-\mu)\geq\mu\gamma\mathrm{err}^{(t+i)}(w^{(i)}), |  |

when μ≤γ\mu\leq\gamma.
For the second term, according to the drift rate assumption, we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗<γ}​μ​(2​γ−μ)≤2​Δ​W​μ​γ\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}<\gamma\}\mu(2\gamma-\mu)\leq 2\Delta W\mu\gamma |  |

For the third term, we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗<γ}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)≥−Δ​W​μ.\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}<\gamma\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right)\geq-\Delta W\mu. |  |

This implies 𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii≥μ​γ​err(t+i)​(w(i))−2​Δ​W​μ\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I\_{i}\geq\mu\gamma\mathrm{err}^{(t+i)}(w^{(i)})-2\Delta W\mu.

By taking a summation for i∈\[T\]i\in\[T\], we have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 1T​∑i=1Terr(t+i)​(w(i))≤4μ​γ​T+1μ​γ​T​∑i=1T(𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii−Ii)+2​Δ​Tγ≤2​Δ1/2​γ−2/3+1μ​γ​T​∑i=1T(𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii−Ii).\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum\_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{err}^{(t+i)}(w^{(i)})\leq\frac{4}{\mu\gamma T}+\frac{1}{\mu\gamma T}\sum\_{i=1}^{T}(\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I\_{i}-I\_{i})+2\frac{\Delta T}{\gamma}\leq 2\Delta^{1/2}\gamma^{-2/3}+\frac{1}{\mu\gamma T}\sum\_{i=1}^{T}(\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I\_{i}-I\_{i}). |  | (13) |

Here, the second inequality follows the choice of μ=γ\mu=\gamma and T=(γ​Δ)−1/2T=(\gamma\Delta)^{-1/2}. It remains to analyze the term 1μ​γ​T​∑i=1T(𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii−Ii)\frac{1}{\mu\gamma T}\sum\_{i=1}^{T}(\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I\_{i}-I\_{i}). To do this, we upper bound the variance of 𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii−Ii\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I\_{i}-I\_{i}, which is at most
We have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii2\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I^{2}\_{i} | =𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗≥γ}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)2\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}\geq\gamma\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right)^{2} |  |
|  |  | +𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)𝟙​{hw(i)​(x)≠y}​𝟙​{y​x⋅w∗<γ}​(2​μ​y​x⋅w∗−μ2)2\displaystyle+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}\mathds{1}\{h\_{w^{(i)}}(x)\neq y\}\mathds{1}\{yx\cdot w^{\*}<\gamma\}\left(2\mu yx\cdot w^{\*}-\mu^{2}\right)^{2} |  |
|  |  | ≤μ2​err(t+i)​(w(i)).\displaystyle\leq\mu^{2}\mathrm{err}^{(t+i)}(w^{(i)}). |  |

Thus, by Bernstein’s inequality, we have with probability at least 1−δ1-\delta,

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | ∑i=1T𝐄(x,y)∼D(t+i)Ii−Ii≤O​(log⁡(1/δ)+∑i=1Tμ2​err(t+i)​(w(i))).\displaystyle\sum\_{i=1}^{T}\operatorname\*{\mathbf{E}}\_{(x,y)\sim D^{(t+i)}}I\_{i}-I\_{i}\leq O\left(\log(1/\delta)+\sqrt{\sum\_{i=1}^{T}\mu^{2}\mathrm{err}^{(t+i)}(w^{(i)})}\right). |  | (14) |

Combining ( [13](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.E13 "Equation 13 ‣ Proof of Theorem˜3.2. ‣ C.5 Proof of Theorem˜3.2 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")) and ( [14](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A3.E14 "Equation 14 ‣ Proof of Theorem˜3.2. ‣ C.5 Proof of Theorem˜3.2 ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs from Section˜3 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")), we have with probability at least 1−δ1-\delta,
1T​∑i=1Terr(t+i)​(w(i))≤O~​(Δ1/2​γ−2/3)\frac{1}{T}\sum\_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{err}^{(t+i)}(w^{(i)})\leq\tilde{O}(\Delta^{1/2}\gamma^{-2/3}).
It remains to show that we are able to select a good hypothesis by looking at the empirical error.

For each j∈\[T\]j\in\[T\], we consider the halfspace h^j=hw(j)\hat{h}\_{j}=h\_{w^{(j)}}. By the assumption on the drift rate, we have \|err((i+1)​W)​(h^j)−err(t+ℓ)​(h^j)\|≤Δ​W=Δ1/2​γ−1/2\left\|\mathrm{err}^{((i+1)W)}(\hat{h}\_{j})-\mathrm{err}^{(t+\ell)}(\hat{h}\_{j})\right\|\leq\Delta W=\Delta^{1/2}\gamma^{-1/2} for every ℓ∈\[W\]\ell\in\[W\], which implies that

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | \|err((i+1)​W)​(h^j)−2W​∑ℓ=1W/2err(t+ℓ+W/2)​(h^j)\|≤Δ​W.\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{err}^{((i+1)W)}(\hat{h}\_{j})-\frac{2}{W}\sum\_{\ell=1}^{W/2}\mathrm{err}^{(t+\ell+W/2)}(\hat{h}\_{j})\right\|\leq\Delta W. |  |

By Hoeffding’s inequality, we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐏𝐫(err^​(h^j)−2W​∑ℓ=1W/2err(t+ℓ+W/2)​(h^j)>err((i+1)​W)​(h^j))≤δ.\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\left(\hat{\mathrm{err}}(\hat{h}\_{j})-\frac{2}{W}\sum\_{\ell=1}^{W/2}\mathrm{err}^{(t+\ell+W/2)}(\hat{h}\_{j})>\mathrm{err}^{((i+1)W)}(\hat{h}\_{j})\right)\leq\delta. |  |

Thus, with probability at least 1−δ1-\delta, we have \|err^​(h^j)−err((i+1)​W)​(h^j)\|≤err((i+1)​W)​(h^j)\left\|\hat{\mathrm{err}}(\hat{h}\_{j})-\mathrm{err}^{((i+1)W)}(\hat{h}\_{j})\right\|\leq\mathrm{err}^{((i+1)W)}(\hat{h}\_{j}).
Since there is some j∗∈\[T\]j^{\*}\in\[T\] such that err(t+j∗)​(w(j∗))≤O~​(Δ1/2​γ−3/2)\mathrm{err}^{(t+j^{\*})}(w^{(j^{\*})})\leq\tilde{O}(\Delta^{1/2}\gamma^{-3/2}), we know that err((i+1)​W)​(w(j∗))≤O~​(Δ1/2​γ−3/2)\mathrm{err}^{((i+1)W)}(w^{(j^{\*})})\leq\tilde{O}(\Delta^{1/2}\gamma^{-3/2}). This implies the selected hypothesis that minimizes err^​(hj)\hat{\mathrm{err}}(h\_{j}) must have err((i+1)​W)​(h^)≤O~​(Δ1/2​γ−3/2)\mathrm{err}^{((i+1)W)}(\hat{h})\leq\tilde{O}(\Delta^{1/2}\gamma^{-3/2}).
∎

## Appendix D Omitted Proofs from [Section˜4](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S4 "4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

### D.1 Proof of [Lemma˜D.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma D.1. ‣ D.1 Proof of Lemma˜D.1 ‣ Appendix D Omitted Proofs from Section˜4 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

###### Lemma D.1.

The instance constructed in [Definition˜4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S4.Thmdefinition3 "Definition 4.3 (Hard Instance). ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") is a valid instance of the trajectory testing problem defined in [Definition˜4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S4.Thmdefinition1 "Definition 4.1 (Trajectory Testing Problem). ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") with η=1/3\eta=1/3.

###### Proof of [Lemma˜D.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#A4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma D.1. ‣ D.1 Proof of Lemma˜D.1 ‣ Appendix D Omitted Proofs from Section˜4 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

To show the construction in [Definition˜4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S4.Thmdefinition3 "Definition 4.3 (Hard Instance). ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise") is a valid instance, we will need to show that for each vv and i∈\[T\]i\in\[T\], the defined halfspaces hi(v)h^{(v)}\_{i} has margin γ\gamma and for every i∈\[T\]i\in\[T\], dT​V​(Di(v),Di+1(v))≤Δd\_{\mathrm{T}V}(D^{(v)}\_{i},D^{(v)}\_{i+1})\leq\Delta.

We start with analyzing the margin. Let hi(v)=sign​(v⋅x+ti),h^{(v)}\_{i}=\mathrm{sign}(v\cdot x+t\_{i}), since v∈{±1}d​zv\in\{\pm 1\}^{d}z, x∈{±1}x\in\{\pm 1\} and dd is odd, we have \|v⋅x\|≥1\left\|v\cdot x\right\|\geq 1. Since (Di(v))x(D^{(v)}\_{i})\_{x} is the uniform distribution over {±1}d\{\pm 1\}^{d}, we know that for any choice of tit\_{i}, there is an integer ti′t\_{i}^{\prime} such that for every x∈{±1}dx\in\{\pm 1\}^{d}, \|v⋅x+ti′\|≥1\left\|v\cdot x+t\_{i}^{\prime}\right\|\geq 1, and

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐏𝐫Di(v)(sign​(v⋅x+ti)=−1)=𝐏𝐫Di(v)(sign​(v⋅x+ti′)=−1)\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}\left(\mathrm{sign}(v\cdot x+t\_{i})=-1\right)=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}\left(\mathrm{sign}(v\cdot x+t\_{i}^{\prime})=-1\right) |  |

This implies that by setting ti=ti′t\_{i}=t\_{i}^{\prime}, halfspace sign​(v⋅x+ti′)\mathrm{sign}(v\cdot x+t\_{i}^{\prime}) has a margin Ω​(1/d)=Ω​(γ)\Omega(1/d)=\Omega(\gamma).

It remains to show that for every i≥T−mi\geq T-m, we have dT​V​(Di(v),Di+1(v))≤Δd\_{\mathrm{T}V}(D^{(v)}\_{i},D^{(v)}\_{i+1})\leq\Delta. Let A⊆{±1}d×{±1}A\subseteq\{\pm 1\}^{d}\times\{\pm 1\} be any event. We have

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐏𝐫Di(v)(A)\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(A) | =𝐏𝐫Di(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)≠hi(v)​(x))+𝐏𝐫Di(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)=hi(v)​(x))\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\neq h^{(v)}\_{i}(x))+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)) |  |
|  |  | =𝐏𝐫Di(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)≠hi(v)​(x))+𝐏𝐫Di(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)=hi(v)​(x)=y​(x))+𝐏𝐫Di(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)=hi(v)​(x)≠y​(x)).\displaystyle=\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\neq h^{(v)}\_{i}(x))+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=y(x))+\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\neq y(x)). |  |

This implies that

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | \|𝐏𝐫Di(v)(A)−𝐏𝐫Di+1(v)(A)\|\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(A)-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i+1}}(A)\right\| | ≤\|𝐏𝐫Di(v)−𝐏𝐫Di+1(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)≠hi(v)​(x))\|+\|𝐏𝐫Di(v)−𝐏𝐫Di+1(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)=hi(v)​(x)=y​(x))\|\displaystyle\leq\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i+1}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\neq h^{(v)}\_{i}(x))\right\|+\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i+1}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=y(x))\right\| |  |
|  |  | +\|𝐏𝐫Di(v)−𝐏𝐫Di+1(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)=hi(v)​(x)≠y​(x))\|\displaystyle+\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i+1}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\neq y(x))\right\| |  |
|  |  | ≤\|𝐏𝐫Di(v)−𝐏𝐫Di+1(v)(hi(v)​(x)≠hi(v)​(x))\|+\|𝐏𝐫Di(v)−𝐏𝐫Di+1(v)(hi(v)​(x)=hi(v)​(x)=y​(x))\|\displaystyle\leq\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i+1}}(h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\neq h^{(v)}\_{i}(x))\right\|+\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i+1}}(h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=y(x))\right\| |  |
|  |  | +\|𝐏𝐫Di(v)−𝐏𝐫Di+1(v)(A,hi(v)​(x)=hi(v)​(x)≠y​(x))\|\displaystyle+\left\|\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}-\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i+1}}(A,h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\neq y(x))\right\| |  |
|  |  | ≤Δ+2​((η−Δ​(j+1))/(1−2​Δ​(j+1))−(η−Δ​j)/(1−2​Δ​j))\displaystyle\leq\Delta+2((\eta-\Delta(j+1))/(1-2\Delta(j+1))-(\eta-\Delta j)/(1-2\Delta j)) |  |
|  |  | ≤4​Δ.\displaystyle\leq 4\Delta. |  |

To conclude the proof of [Lemma˜D.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#A4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma D.1. ‣ D.1 Proof of Lemma˜D.1 ‣ Appendix D Omitted Proofs from Section˜4 ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), it remains to show that we can actually make
𝐏𝐫Di(v)(hi(v)​(x)=−1)=Δ​j\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=-1)=\Delta j by showing that changing the threshold tt will not make 𝐏𝐫Di(v)(hi(v)​(x)=−1)\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=-1) dramatically change. Since v∈{±1}dv\in\{\pm 1\}^{d} and x∈{±1}dx\in\{\pm 1\}^{d}, we know that v​cot⁡x∈\[−d,d\]v\cot x\in\[-d,d\] is an integer. Since (Di(v))x(D^{(v)}\_{i})\_{x} is a uniform distribution over {±1}\{\pm 1\}, we know that for every integer cc,

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | 𝐏𝐫(Di(v))x(v⋅x=c)≤(dd/2)/2d≤2−O​(d).\displaystyle\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(D^{(v)}\_{i})\_{x}}(v\cdot x=c)\leq\binom{d}{d/2}/2^{d}\leq 2^{-O(d)}. |  |

This implies that as long as γ<O​(log−1⁡(1/Δ))\gamma<O(\log^{-1}(1/\Delta)), we can make 𝐏𝐫(Di(v))x(v⋅x=c)≤Δ/100\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{(D^{(v)}\_{i})\_{x}}(v\cdot x=c)\leq\Delta/100. This implies that we are able to choose hi(v)h^{(v)}\_{i} such that 𝐏𝐫Di(v)(hi(v)​(x)=−1)=Δ​j\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=-1)=\Delta j.
∎

### D.2 Proof of [Lemma˜4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 4.1. ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

###### Lemma D.2(Restatement of [Lemma˜4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 4.1. ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")).

Under the construction of [Definition˜4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S4.Thmdefinition3 "Definition 4.3 (Hard Instance). ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), we have

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | ⟨(D(u)¯≤ℓ,k),(D(v)¯≤ℓ,k)⟩D(0)−1=∑A⊆\[m\],A≠∅,\|A\|≤k∏j∈\[A\]⟨(DT−m+j(u)¯)≤ℓ(zi)),(DT−m+j(v)¯)≤ℓ(zi)⟩Di(0)−1\displaystyle\langle(\bar{D^{(u)}}^{\leq\ell,k}),(\bar{D^{(v)}}^{\leq\ell,k})\rangle\_{D^{(0)}}-1=\sum\_{A\subseteq\[m\],A\neq\emptyset,\|A\|\leq k}\prod\_{j\in\[A\]}\langle(\bar{D^{(u)}\_{T-m+j}})^{\leq\ell}(z\_{i})),(\bar{D^{(v)}\_{T-m+j}})^{\leq\ell}(z\_{i})\rangle\_{D\_{i}^{(0)}}-1 |  |

###### Proof of [Lemma˜4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 4.1. ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

Since Di(u)D^{(u)}\_{i}
According to the construction of D(0)D^{(0)} and 𝒟\mathcal{D}, for every u∈{±1}du\in\{\pm 1\}^{d},
we have D(u)¯=∏i=1TDi(u)¯​(zi)=∏i=T−m+1TDi(u)¯​(zi)\bar{D^{(u)}}=\prod\_{i=1}^{T}\bar{D^{(u)}\_{i}}(z\_{i})=\prod\_{i=T-m+1}^{T}\bar{D^{(u)}\_{i}}(z\_{i}).

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

With [Lemma˜4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma 4.1. ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), it remains to bound the pairwise correlation.

### D.3 Proof of [Lemma˜4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S4.Thmlemma2 "Lemma 4.2. ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")

###### Lemma D.3(Restatement of [Lemma˜4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S4.Thmlemma2 "Lemma 4.2. ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise")).

Let c∈(0,1/2)c\in(0,1/2) be a constant. For i=T−m+j,j∈\[m\]i=T-m+j,j\in\[m\] and v,u∈{±1}dv,u\in\{\pm 1\}^{d} such that \|v⋅u\|≤d1−c\left\|v\cdot u\right\|\leq d^{1-c}, we have

###### Proof of [Lemma˜4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1\#S4.Thmlemma2 "Lemma 4.2. ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise").

Recall that in [Definition˜4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11149v1#S4.Thmdefinition3 "Definition 4.3 (Hard Instance). ‣ Hardness Construction ‣ 4 Low-Degree Polynomial Hardness ‣ Efficiently Learning Drifting Halfspaces with Massart Noise"), for every i=T−m+j,j∈\[m\]i=T-m+j,j\in\[m\] and for every u∈{±1}du\in\{\pm 1\}^{d}, D(u)D^{{}^{(u)}} is realized by halfspaces hi(u)=sign​(v⋅x+ti)h^{(u)}\_{i}=\mathrm{sign}(v\cdot x+t\_{i}) with RCN ηi=(η−Δ​j)/(1−2​Δ​j)\eta\_{i}=(\eta-\Delta j)/(1-2\Delta j). In particular, 𝐏𝐫Di(v)(hi(v)​(x)=−1)=Δ​j\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=-1)=\Delta j.
This implies that

|  |  | =ηi+(1−2​ηi)​𝐏𝐫Di(v)(hi(v)​(x)=−1)\displaystyle=\eta\_{i}+(1-2\eta\_{i})\operatorname\*{\mathbf{Pr}}\_{D^{(v)}\_{i}}(h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=-1) |  |
|  |  | =η−Δ​j1−2​Δ​j+(1−2​η−Δ​j1−2​Δ​j)​Δ​j=η.\displaystyle=\frac{\eta-\Delta j}{1-2\Delta j}+(1-2\frac{\eta-\Delta j}{1-2\Delta j})\Delta j=\eta. |  |

Since Di(0)D^{(0)}\_{i} is a product distribution over {±1}d×{±1}\{\pm 1\}^{d}\times\{\pm 1\} and (Di(0))x(D^{(0)}\_{i})\_{x} is the uniform distribution over {±1}d\{\pm 1\}^{d}, we have

|  | =\displaystyle= | η​(∑x∈{±1}dDi(u)​(x)∣y=−1​Di(v)​(x)∣y=−1Di(0)(x)∣y=−1)−1)+(1−η)​(∑x∈{±1}dDi(u)​(x)∣y=1​Di(v)​(x)∣y=1Di(0)(x)∣y=1)−1)\displaystyle\eta(\sum\_{x\in\{\pm 1\}^{d}}\frac{D^{(u)}\_{i}(x)\mid\_{y=-1}D^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\mid\_{y=-1}}{D^{(0)}\_{i}(x)\mid\_{y=-1})}-1)+(1-\eta)(\sum\_{x\in\{\pm 1\}^{d}}\frac{D^{(u)}\_{i}(x)\mid\_{y=1}D^{(v)}\_{i}(x)\mid\_{y=1}}{D^{(0)}\_{i}(x)\mid\_{y=1})}-1) |  |

|  | =\displaystyle= | ⟨ηi+𝟙​{hi(v)​(x)=−1}​(1−2​ηi),ηi+𝟙​{hi(v)​(x)=−1}​(1−2​ηi)⟩Di(0)/η2−1\displaystyle\langle\eta\_{i}+\mathds{1}\{h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=-1\}(1-2\eta\_{i}),\eta\_{i}+\mathds{1}\{h^{(v)}\_{i}(x)=-1\}(1-2\eta\_{i})\rangle\_{D\_{i}^{(0)}}/\eta^{2}-1 |  |
